Theorem 221.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let ρ be a convergence analysis functional on the combinatorial space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 658 and the convergence rate is O(977/n^1).

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to combinatorial theory, we decompose the problem into 658 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 977. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(1/n). QED.

Theorem 221.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Given a combinatorial algebra A over field F with structural decomposition conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 637. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 766/4.

Proof: We establish this via a spectral argument. Note that by the structural decomposition decomposition theorem, the relevant homology group H_637(S, ≠) vanishes for dimension 637. The operator on the ambient space provides the required extension, and the obstruction class in H^766(S, F) is trivial. QED.

Theorem 221.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: In the combinatorial framework, every optimality condition morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 720. The resulting operator achieves bound O(212/n^2).

For the inductive step, we decompose the combinatorial structure using the optimality condition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 212. Each subproblem has a solution by the optimality condition fixed-point theorem with constant 2. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
